The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> is used in this study. A cross has been performed to study the linkage relationships among two genes, and the resulting genotypes are summarized in the table below.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #944226;">Gene C</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #944226;"><i>clumpy</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #944226;">Gene C</span> grows in dense, irregular clusters, with cells clumping together rather than spreading smoothly.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #2e5f9e;">Gene R</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #2e5f9e;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #2e5f9e;">Gene R</span> colonies develop a reddish-brown pigmentation, reminiscent of rusted metal.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> is used in this study. A cross has been performed to study the linkage relationships among two genes, and the resulting genotypes are summarized in the table below.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #884d13;">Gene D</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #884d13;"><i>doubled</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #884d13;">Gene D</span> cells display double or multiple budding, with several buds emerging simultaneously.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #6d46a6;">Gene R</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #6d46a6;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #6d46a6;">Gene R</span> colonies develop a reddish-brown pigmentation, reminiscent of rusted metal.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; MC	<p>8b50_d7e7</p> <h5>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Mapping</h5><p>In this problem, you will use unordered tetrads to determine the between a single pair of genes and calculate the distances between them. The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> is used in this study. A cross has been performed to study the linkage relationships among two genes, and the resulting genotypes are summarized in the table below.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #695f00;">Gene F</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #695f00;"><i>fuzzy</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #695f00;">Gene F</span> colonies are covered in soft, fine filaments, giving them a fuzzy, cotton-like texture.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00697b;">Gene P</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #00697b;"><i>pebble</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00697b;">Gene P</span> produces colonies with a rough, uneven surface that resembles a collection of tiny pebbles.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; MC	<p>f64c_e19b</p> <h5>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Mapping</h5><p>In this problem, you will use unordered tetrads to determine the between a single pair of genes and calculate the distances between them. The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> is used in this study. A cross has been performed to study the linkage relationships among two genes, and the resulting genotypes are summarized in the table below.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #7a5705;">Gene A</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #7a5705;"><i>amber</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #873792;">Gene D</span> cells display double or multiple budding, with several buds emerging simultaneously.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> is used in this study.
